11: This document explains the role of the principal structures used to preserve

24: ## Structure Map

--- lines 26-40 ---
26: | Structure | Role | What It Preserves |
27: |---|---|---|
28: | Session | Continuous interval of work | Beginning, end, purpose, and transformations produced during the session |
29: | Transformation | Primary unit of evolution | Need → Research → Decision → Implementation → Result |
30: | Persistent Experience | Raw lived experience | Conversation, Bash, terminal execution, observations, and reflection |
31: | Evidence | Proof of what actually occurred | Outputs, tests, files, Git state, runtime observations, and other verifiable evidence |
32: | Witness | Witness of a transformation | Compact persistent proof that a transformation occurred and what resulted from it |
33: | Trace | Current position in the evolutionary process | Latest transformation and observed state |
34: | Lineage | Genealogy of evolution | Relationships between successive transformations and their historical ancestry |
35: | Memory | Sedimented knowledge | Knowledge that the organism must preserve beyond the session in which it was acquired |
36: | CSL / Living Project Image | Current demonstrable truth | The current epistemic representation of what the project demonstrably is |
37:

1610: # 0.6 — Layered Epistemic Memory
1611: 
1612: Version: 0.1.0
1613: 
1614: Status: CANON
1615: 
1616: Classification: Epistemic Continuity — Memory
1617: 
1618: ## Purpose
1619: 
1620: This section defines the current research model for how AI-Toolkit may
1621: preserve very large amounts of experience without requiring all preserved
1622: information to remain continuously present in active AI context.
1623: 
1624: The organism must be capable of preserving years of evolution while recalling
1625: only the knowledge relevant to the current purpose.
1626: 
1627: The central idea is:
1628: 
1629: **Preservation does not mean permanent cognitive loading.**
1630: 
1631: The organism may preserve very large amounts of experience while bringing
1632: only relevant knowledge into active context.
1633: 
1634: ---
1635: 
1636: ## 0.6.1 — Memory Depth
1637: 
1638: The current research model distinguishes progressively different levels of
1639: epistemic depth.
1640:

1645:       ▼

1649:       ↕

1651:       ↕

1653:       ↕

1655:       ↕

1671: ## 0.6.2 — Meaning Increases Upward, Detail Increases Downward
1672:

1674: 
1675: - informational volume should generally decrease;
1676: - semantic concentration should increase;
1677: - current relevance should increase;

1690: meaning and the smaller the information volume.**
1691: 
1692: **The deeper we travel, the greater the contextual detail until we reach the
1693: original experience and Evidence.**
1694:

1733: ## 0.6.4 — Epistemic Sedimentation
1734:

1737: manner into Memory and Knowledge.

1740: 
1741: ORIGINAL EXPERIENCE

1755:

1757: organism must remain capable of returning to the original experience.

1761: ## 0.6.5 — Non-Duplicative Memory Principle
1762: 
1763: Higher memory levels should preserve meaning, relationships, and learned
1764: knowledge rather than unnecessarily duplicating lower-level experience.
1765: 
1766: For example, a long research conversation may produce a concise semantic
1767: Memory.
1768: 
1769: The concise Memory should preserve its relationship to the research from
1770: which it originated.
1771: 
1772: Therefore:
1773: 
1774: **Memory should preserve what was learned, not simply copy everything that
1775: was experienced.**
1776: 
1777: ---

1784: The organism should not depend entirely upon an AI deciding:
1785:

1803: ## 0.6.7 — Memory Stability Levels
1804: 
1805: The research currently distinguishes several possible memory roles.
1806: 
1807: ### Working Memory
1808: 
1809: Information required for the current activity.
1810: 
1811: Example:
1812: 
1813: "We are currently researching layered epistemic memory."
1814: 
1815: ### Episodic Memory
1816: 
1817: What happened during a particular experience.
1818: 
1819: Example:
1820: 
1821: "During Epistemic Continuity research, the Owner identified uncontrolled
1822: memory growth as a future problem."
1823: 
1824: ### Semantic Memory
1825: 
1826: Meaning sedimented from experience.
1827: 
1828: Example:
1829: 
1830: "Preservation does not require permanent cognitive loading."
1831: 
1832: ### Established Knowledge
1833: 
1834: Understanding that has been sufficiently supported and accepted for the
1835: relevant epistemic purpose.
1836: 
1837: Example:
1838: 
1839: "The project must carry its own continuity."
1840: 
1841: ### Canonical Knowledge
1842: 
1843: Knowledge that has passed through the required canonical governance process
1844: and possesses canonical authority.
1845: 
1846: These categories are research concepts.
1847: 
1848: They are not yet finalized as canonical storage structures.
1849: 
1850: ---

5150:

6012: ## 0.8.41 — Living Project Image Integrity Principle
6013: 
6014: ### Living Project Image Integrity Principle
6015: 
6016: The Living Project Image shall represent the best-supported current project reality and shall expose uncertainty, contradiction, absence, staleness, regression, and divergence rather than silently filling those conditions with assumptions.
6017: 
6018: This is one of the strongest applications of Reality First and EXIST within the continuity model.
6019: 
6020: A truthful incomplete image is superior to a complete fictional image.

6087: 
6088: This continues the memory-efficiency principles established earlier.

10573: ## PCC-03 — Provenance + Lineage — Canonical Admission
10574:

10576:

10578: Canon after successful implementation and production-readiness examination.
10579: 
10580: The canonical executable epistemic continuity established by PCC-03 is:

10583:

10585: 
10586: - provenance has stable epistemic identity;
10587: - observations remain connected to their sources;
10588: - evidence remains connected to observations;
10589: - claims remain connected to explicit evidence;
10590: - verification remains connected to claims;

10594:   historical Knowledge;
10595: - provenance is navigable both forward and backward;
10596: - persistence and reconstruction preserve epistemic identity and relations;

10598: - unknown or dangling reality must remain explicit rather than invented;
10599: - Human Authority remains distinguishable from technical evidence;
10600: - PCC-03 does not create a parallel Memory organ;
10601: - PCC-03 does not create a parallel Transformation organ;
10602: - PCC-03 does not itself claim to implement the complete CSL / Living Project
10603:   Image.
10604:

10606: 
10607: **CSL / Living Project Image — current demonstrable truth.**

10610: language based on `*.csl`, EngineeringCompiler, parser/compiler machinery, or

10612: meaning.
10613: 
10614: PCC-03 implementation mechanisms may evolve, but future evolution must preserve
10615: these canonical semantic and epistemic invariants unless Human Authority
10616: explicitly changes the Canon.

10: # 1. Canonical Identity
11:

13:

15:

30: 
31: # 2. Canonical Meaning
32: 
33: Persistent Experience is the project-owned preservation of epistemically significant lived project experience in a form that retains sufficient original context, sequence, provenance, identity, relations, and execution reality for later humans and AI collaborators to understand what occurred without depending upon manual reconstruction by the original participant.
34: 
35: Persistent Experience is not merely a log.
36: 
37: It is not merely terminal history.
38: 
39: It is not merely conversation history.
40: 
41: It is not merely a final artifact.
42: 
43: It preserves the epistemically meaningful path through which the organism and Human Authority lived, examined, corrected, executed, observed, and reached a resulting state.
44: 
45: ---
46: 
47: # 3. Canonical Purpose
48: 
49: Persistent Experience exists to prevent epistemic loss.
50: 
51: A project may preserve code, commits, documents, tests, Evidence, terminal output, and current state while still losing the lived path that explains how and why those things came to exist.
52: 
53: Persistent Experience preserves sufficient continuity so that later understanding does not depend upon reconstructing history from disconnected artifacts or upon the original human manually retelling the past.
54: 
55: ---
56: 
57: # 4. Experience and Session

--- lines 67-91 ---
67: An Experience may relate to a Session.
68: 
69: That relationship does not collapse their identities.
70: 
71: Process death does not by itself erase the identity of a persistent Experience.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: # 5. Experience and Memory
76: 
77: Experience and Memory are distinct.
78: 
79: **Experience != Memory**
80: 
81: Experience may contribute to Memory.
82: 
83: Memory may preserve what should remain learned from Experience.
84: 
85: Memory does not become Experience merely because it refers to Experience.
86: 
87: Experience does not become Memory merely because it persists.
88:

90:

181: Persistence must preserve sufficient identity, provenance, relations, and state for legitimate recovery.
182: 
183: Persistence alone does not create epistemic authority.
184: 
185: **Persistence != authority**

--- lines 199-207 ---
199: ---
200: 
201: # 14. Capture Before Interpretation
202: 
203: Epistemically significant Experience must be preservable before later interpretation, compression, sedimentation, summarization, Witness formation, Memory formation, CSL representation, or contextual packaging becomes the only surviving account.
204: 
205: Interpretation may occur.
206: 
207: Compression may occur.

--- lines 213-221 ---
213: ---
214: 
215: # 15. Historical Integrity
216: 
217: Later knowledge must not silently rewrite original Experience.
218: 
219: A failed command remains historically a failed command.
220: 
221: A rejected proposal remains historically a rejected proposal.

--- lines 245-253 ---
245: ---
246: 
247: # 17. Execution Reality
248: 
249: Where technically observable and epistemically significant, Persistent Experience must be capable of relating to execution reality including applicable information such as:
250: 
251: - executed command or command stream;
252: - stdout;
253: - stderr;

--- lines 284-294 ---
284: Interpretation must not silently become raw Evidence.
285: 
286: ---
287: 
288: # 19. Provenance
289: 
290: Persistent Experience must preserve sufficient provenance to explain, where applicable:
291: 
292: - who contributed something;
293: - what produced it;
294: - when it occurred;

--- lines 298-324 ---
298: - which Evidence supports relevant claims;
299: - which artifact resulted;
300: - whether a representation is original or derived.
301: 
302: Provenance available at capture time must not later be replaced by assumption.
303: 
304: ---
305: 
306: # 20. Artifact Relations
307: 
308: Persistent Experience may associate resulting artifacts with the Experience that produced or materially changed them.
309: 
310: Such artifacts may include source files, research, audits, Evidence, tests, commits, configuration changes, CSL, Witnesses, and Transformations.
311: 
312: Association establishes provenance.
313: 
314: Association does not grant the Experience authority over the canonical status of the related artifact.
315: 
316: ---
317: 
318: # 21. Protection
319: 
320: Persistent Experience must be protected against illegitimate access, contamination between contexts, uncontrolled modification, unjustified exposure, and recovery into the wrong identity.
321: 
322: Persistence without adequate protection is insufficient.
323: 
324: Protection must preserve the distinction between Experience and the authorities that may act upon it.

--- lines 326-334 ---
326: ---
327: 
328: # 22. Privacy
329: 
330: Persistent Experience must respect legitimate privacy boundaries.
331: 
332: Persistence does not justify indiscriminate capture or exposure.
333: 
334: The organism must preserve only what is legitimate for the relevant Experience and must prevent unnecessary propagation of protected material across epistemic boundaries.

--- lines 356-364 ---
356: ---
357: 
358: # 25. Conflict and Ambiguity
359: 
360: Persistent Experience must not invent certainty when preserved representations conflict.
361: 
362: Conflict must remain explicitly representable.
363: 
364: Ambiguity must remain visible when available reality is ambiguous.

--- lines 380-388 ---
380: ---
381: 
382: # 27. Coordination and Concurrency
383: 
384: Concurrent or coordinated interaction with Persistent Experience must preserve identity and integrity.
385: 
386: Coordination mechanisms must not silently create multiple contradictory historical identities for one Experience.
387: 
388: When conflict cannot be safely resolved, the organism must preserve the conflict or refuse unsafe continuation rather than inventing certainty.

--- lines 402-420 ---
402: ---
403: 
404: # 29. Backup and Recovery
405: 
406: Operational preservation mechanisms may back up and recover Persistent Experience.
407: 
408: Backup copies do not become independent Experiences merely because they are physically distinct representations.
409: 
410: Recovery must preserve identity, integrity, provenance, and applicable protection boundaries.
411: 
412: ---
413: 
414: # 30. Operational Observability
415: 
416: The organism must be able to observe the operational health of Persistent Experience sufficiently to determine whether persistence, recovery, protection, and related physiology are functioning.
417: 
418: Operational observability must not redefine Experience.
419: 
420: Telemetry about Experience is not the Experience itself.

--- lines 422-430 ---
422: ---
423: 
424: # 31. Performance
425: 
426: Persistent Experience must remain operationally usable at the scale for which the organism declares it production-ready.
427: 
428: Performance characterization is Evidence about implementation behavior.
429: 
430: Performance results do not redefine canonical meaning.

--- lines 432-440 ---
432: ---
433: 
434: # 32. Deployment Behavior
435: 
436: Persistent Experience must preserve its required identity and persistence behavior across legitimate deployment boundaries for which production readiness is claimed.
437: 
438: Deployment infrastructure is not Experience.
439: 
440: Deployment changes must not silently redefine the epistemic identity of preserved Experience.

--- lines 442-466 ---

445:

447: 
448: Safe refusal is preferable to false continuity.

482: # 36. Canonical Justification
483: 
484: Persistent Experience is canonical because epistemic continuity is a requirement of the organism rather than an incidental property of one implementation.
485: 
486: Without it, the organism may retain products of its history while losing the lived epistemic path required to understand those products.
487: 
488: Persistent Experience therefore protects continuity between historical reality, later Evidence, Human judgment, Memory, future interpretation, and continued organism evolution while preserving the distinctions between those functions.
